[&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div id="content_div_183649_389227"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">This section reports on the performance of primary and community health services which include general practice, pharmaceutical services, dentistry, allied health services, community health services, maternal and child health and alcohol and other drug treatment. This section does not include:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 href="/ongoing/report-on-government-services/health/public-hospitals/">section 12</a>, ‘Public hospitals’)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">community mental health services (reported in <a class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 font-normal text-blue-dark border-transparent hover:text-blue-mid hover:underline active:bg-grey-200 text-base" id="section-13" href="/ongoing/report-on-government-services/health/services-for-mental-health/">section 13</a>, ‘Services for mental health’)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">home and community care services (reported in <a class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The<strong> Indicator results</strong> tab uses data from the data tables to provide information on the performance for each indicator in the <strong>Indicator framework.</strong> The same data is also available in CSV format.</p></div></div></div><div class="mx-auto container p-0 bg-grey-15 pt-5 pb-9 space-y-9 border-t-4 border-t-blue-dark class="font-sans text-heading-l font-medium mb-2">Data downloads</h2><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 </li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2 list-none -ml-4"><a href="https://assets.pc.gov.au/2026-01/rogs-2026-parte-section10-primary-and-community-health-dataset_0.csv?VersionId=skSVcIjTxjuNWGWtKhp75L9zKdEohB9W" class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 font-normal text-blue-dark border-transparent hover:text-blue-mid hover:underline active:bg-grey-200 text-base" id="10-primary-and-community-health-dataset-csv---33-mb"><span class="inline-flex gap-1"><img src="/icons/csv-icon.png" alt="CSV document" class="w-5 h-5 noprint-inline"/>10 Primary and community health dataset (CSV - 3.3 MB)</span></a>
</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Refer to the corresponding table number in the data tables for detailed definitions, caveats, footnotes and data source(s).</p></div></div></div></div><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2 list-none -ml-4"><a href="https://assets.pc.gov.au/2026-01/how-to-use-RoGS.pdf?VersionId=49AsBI0gQCLPTIy94vPhXwJ26NHMDyRC" class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 font-normal text-blue-dark border-transparent hover:text-blue-mid hover:underline active:bg-grey-200 text-base" id="guide-how-to-find-what-you-need-in-rogs-pdf---8129-kb"><span class="inline-flex gap-1"><img src="/icons/pdf-icon.png" alt="PDF document" class="w-5 h-5 noprint-inline"/>Guide: How to find what you need in RoGS (PDF - 812.9 KB)</span></a>
</li></ul></div></div><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="w-full"><div class="relative"><div class="after:absolute after:right-0 after:top-0 after:w-[4.4rem] after:h-full before:bg-gradient-to-r before:from-white before:z-[1] before:to-transparent [&amp;[data-scroll-left=false]]:before:hidden"><div class="flex [&amp;[data-scroll=true]]:overflow-x-scroll"><button id="context" class="py-3 px-5 text-button-s relative transition-colors rounded-t-lg border-2 focus:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-blue-mid text-grey-dark font-semibold bg-grey-15 border-grey-light border-b-0" aria-selected="true" role="tab" data-tab="context">Context</button><button id="indicator-framework" class="py-3 px-5 text-button-s hover:underline border-grey/25 border-b-0" aria-selected="false" role="tab" data-tab="indicator-results">Indicator results</button><button id="aboriginal-and-torres-strait-islander-data" class="py-3 hover:text-grey-dark hover:underline border-grey/25 border-b-0" aria-selected="false" role="tab" data-tab="aboriginal-and-torres-strait-islander-data">Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander data</button><button id="explanatory-material" class="py-3 px-5 text-button-s font-medium relative transition-colors rounded-t-lg border-2 focus:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 data-tab="explanatory-material">Explanatory material</button></div></div></div><div class="p-5 bg-grey-15 border-b-blue-dark border-b-[6px]"><div role="tabpanel" class="inline" aria-hidden="false" aria-labelledby="context"><h3 class="printonly mt-8 mb-4 font-bold text-4xl text-blue-dark print-tab-title">Context</h3><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div><h3 class="font-sans text-heading-m mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="objectives-for-primary-and-community-health">Objectives for primary and community health</h3><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Primary and community health services aim to promote health, prevent illness and support people to manage their health issues in the community, by providing services that are:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">timely, affordable and accessible to all</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">appropriate and responsive to meet the needs of individuals throughout their lifespan and communities</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">well coordinated to ensure continuity of care where more than one service type, and/or ongoing service provision is required</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">sustainable.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Governments aim for primary and community health services to meet these objectives in an equitable and efficient manner.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words hidenojs"></p><div class="indicators"> </div></div></div></div><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="flex justify-end mb-2"><button class="break-words font-semibold font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 text-button-s px-1 py-1 rounded-md bg-white inline-flex text-blue-dark border-blue-mid hover:text-blue-dark active:text-blue-dark focus:text-blue-dark disabled:text-grey [&amp;&gt;i]:text-blue-dark [&amp;&gt;i]:shrink-0 hover:bg-grey-dark/5 noprint-inline">Expand All</button></div><div class="bg-white" data-block="true" data-orientation="vertical"><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_2uhkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="service-overview" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">Service overview</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_2uhkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_uhkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Primary and community health services are delivered by a range of health and allied health professionals across private, not‑for‑profit and government service settings. Definitions for common health terms are provided in the &#x27;Explanatory material&#x27; tab.</p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="general-practice">General practice</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">General practice is a major provider of primary healthcare in Australia. Services include preventative care, the diagnosis and treatment of illness and injury, delivered directly and/or through referral to acute (hospital) or other healthcare services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The Australian Government provides the majority of general practice income, through Services Australia – mainly as fee-for-service payments via the Medicare Benefits Schedule (Medicare) – and the Department of Veterans’ Affairs (DVA). Additional funding is provided to influence the supply, regional distribution and quality of general practice services, and support engagement of the health workforce in primary health care settings including residential aged care. This includes initiatives such as the Practice Incentives Program, the Workforce Incentive Program, the General Practice in Aged Care Incentive and Primary Health Networks (Services Australia 2025). State and territory governments also provide some funding for such programs, mainly to influence the availability of General Practitioners (GPs) in rural and remote areas. Other funding sources include insurance schemes (for example, workers compensation and traffic accident schemes) and patient contributions.</p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="pharmaceutical-services">Pharmaceutical services</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The Australian Government funds the Pharmaceutical Benefits Scheme (PBS), which subsidises the cost of many medicines in Australia. The PBS schedule sets a price for listed medicines and a maximum co-payment amount that people contribute towards the cost of these medicines. The Australian Government incurs the expense of any difference where the listed price exceeds the patient co-payment (whether for general or concessional patients).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">In 2022-23, around 69% of PBS prescriptions dispensed were above the co-payment threshold. Patients paid the co-payment and Australian Government subsidised the remainder. Around 31% were below the threshold, meaning the patient paid the full cost with no government subsidy (AIHW 2024a). Co-payments contribute to a patient’s safety net threshold that, once reached, provides eligibility to receive PBS medicines at a lower cost or free of charge (DHDA 2025a).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The Repatriation Pharmaceutical Benefits Scheme (RPBS) provides subsidised pharmaceutical medicines, dressings and other items to war veterans and war widows. The RPBS is administered by the DVA.</p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="dental-services">Dental services</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Australia has a mixed system of public and private dental services. State and territory governments deliver public dental services, which are primarily available to children and disadvantaged adults. The Australian Government works with state and territory governments to fund dental services. Since 2013, the Australian Government has increased funding for public dental services via National Partnership Agreements and Federal Funding Agreements with states and territories and the Child Dental Benefits Schedule. The private sector receives funding to provide some public dental services from the Australian Government through the DVA and the Child Dental Benefits Schedule, and from state and territory governments through dental voucher systems. The Australian Government is working with states and territories to develop the next ten-year <em>National Oral Health Plan</em> which will set out priorities to improve dental health across Australia (DHDA 2025b). Data on dental service expenditure for the years 2014-15 to 2023-24 is presented in table 10A.6. </p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="allied-health-services">Allied health services</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Allied health services include, but are not limited to, physiotherapy, psychology, occupational therapy, podiatry and osteopathy. They are delivered mainly in the private sector. Some government funding of private allied health services is provided through insurance schemes and the private health insurance rebate. The Australian Government makes some allied health services available under Medicare to patients with particular needs – for example, people with chronic conditions and complex care needs. The Australian Government also funds the Workforce Incentive Program – Practice Steam, which supports general practices, Aboriginal Medical Services and Aboriginal Community Controlled Health Services with the cost of engaging eligible allied health professionals. Data on the number of Medicare rebated allied health services used per person and the availability of public allied health professionals by region is presented in tables 10A.10 and 10A.22, respectively.</p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="community-health-services">Community health services</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Community health services generally comprise multidisciplinary teams of health and allied health professionals who provide targeted health promotion, prevention and management services. Their aim is to protect the health and wellbeing of local populations, particularly people who have or are at risk of the poorest health and/or have the greatest economic and social needs, while taking pressure off the acute care health system. Governments (including local governments) provide community health services directly or indirectly by funding local health services and community organisations. There is no national strategy for community health services and service provision varies across jurisdictions.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">State and territory governments are responsible for most community health services. The Australian Government is primarily responsible for serving Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander communities, with state and territory governments also providing some funding.</p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="maternal-and-child-health-services">Maternal and child health services</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Maternal and child health services are funded by state and territory governments. They provide services including: parenting support (including antenatal and postnatal programs); early childhood nursing programs; disease prevention programs (including childhood immunisations); and early intervention and treatment programs related to child health and development. Some jurisdictions also provide specialist programs through child health services, including hearing screening programs, and mothers and babies residential programs.</p><h4 class="font-sans text-heading-s mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="alcohol-and-other-drug-treatment">Alcohol and other drug treatment</h4><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Alcohol and other drug treatment activities range from a brief intervention to long-term residential treatment. Types of treatment include withdrawal management, pharmacological treatment, counselling and rehabilitation. Selected data on these services is available in table 10A.13.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_3ehkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="funding" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">Funding</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_3ehkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_1ehkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">In 2023-24, of the $51.5 billion government recurrent expenditure on primary and community health services, almost three-quarters was funded by the Australian Government and one-quarter by state, territory and local governments (table 10A.1). This included: </p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">$12.8 billion for community health services (16.5% by the Australian Government and 83.5% by state, territory and local governments)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">$2.5 billion for dental services (57.9% by the Australian Government and 42.1% by state, territory and local governments) (table 10A.6).</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Where more recent data is available, for 2024-25, Australian Government expenditure was:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">$13.4 billion on general practice (table 10A.2)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">$19.5 billion through the PBS and RPBS on Section 85 prescription medicines filled at pharmacies (table 10A.3). Expenditure by type of service is available in table 10A.4</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">$70.5 million on funding of PBS medicines to Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander primary health services in remote and very remote areas (table 10A.5)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">$981.0 million on Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander primary health services (table 10A.7).</li></ul></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_3uhkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="size-and-scope" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">Size and scope</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_3uhkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_1uhkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Nationally in 2024, there were 40,375 GPs – 29,976 on a full-time equivalent (FTE) basis, equating to 110.2 GPs per 100,000 people (table 10A.8), similar to 109.7 in 2023. This remains lower than the peak in 2021 (120.9) (table 10A.8).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Nationally in 2024-25, rates of GP-type services used per person was 6.4 services per person, similar to 2023-24 (6.3 services per person) (figure 10.1). GP-type services disaggregated by in-person attendances and telehealth appointments are reported in table 10A.9. Nationally in 2024-25, 1.0 telehealth GP-type service was used per person (table 10A.9).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">In 2024-25, around 226 million services (8.2 per person) were subsidised under Section 85 of the PBS with 87.0% provided to concessional patients. A further 6.9 million services were subsidised under the RPBS (tables 10A.11–12).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Nationally in 2023-24 there were:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">1,304 alcohol and other drug treatment agencies (31.2% identified as government providers) with a reported 240,958 closed treatment episodes (26.8% identified as government provided) (table 10A.13).</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">213 Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander primary health services which provided 3.8 million episodes of healthcare (table 10A.14). Data by remoteness is presented in table 10A.15 and health service staffing numbers are provided in table 10A.16.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The most recent available data on public dental service usage (2021) showed that nationally, 11% of adults aged 18 years or over visited a public dental practice for their last dental visit (AIHW 2024b).</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div role="tabpanel" class="hidden printonly" aria-hidden="true" aria-labelledby="indicator-framework"><h3 class="printonly mt-8 mb-4 font-bold text-4xl text-blue-dark print-tab-title">Indicator Framework</h3><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The performance indicator framework provides information on equity, effectiveness and efficiency, and distinguishes the outputs and outcomes of primary and community health services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The performance indicator framework shows which data is complete and comparable in this report. For data that is not considered directly comparable, text includes relevant caveats and supporting commentary. <a class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 font-normal text-blue-dark border-transparent hover:text-blue-mid hover:underline active:bg-grey-200 text-base" id="section-1" href="/ongoing/report-on-government-services/approach/performance-measurement/">Section 1</a> discusses data comparability and completeness from a report-wide perspective. In addition to the contextual information for this service area (refer to Context tab), the report&#x27;s statistical context (<a class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 font-normal text-blue-dark border-transparent hover:text-blue-mid hover:underline active:bg-grey-200 text-base" id="section-2" href="/ongoing/report-on-government-services/approach/statistical-context/">section 2</a>) contains data that may assist in interpreting the performance indicators presented in this section.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Improvements to performance reporting for primary and community health services are ongoing and include identifying data sources to fill gaps in reporting for performance indicators and measures, and improving the comparability and completeness of data.</p><h3 class="font-sans text-heading-m mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="outputs">Outputs</h3><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Outputs are the services delivered (while outcomes are the impact of these services on the status of an individual or group) (refer to section 1). Output information is also critical for equitable, efficient and effective management of government services.</p><h3 class="font-sans text-heading-m mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="outcomes">Outcomes</h3><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Outcomes are the impact of services on the status of an individual or group (refer to section 1).</p><article class="align-center media media--type-image media--view-mode-default">

complete</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Efficiency</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans and complete</li></ul><h5 class="font-sans text-heading-xs mb-4 mt-8 font-normal text-grey-dark [&amp;+[data-block]]:mt-4" id="outcomes">Outcomes</h5><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Notifications of selected childhood diseases – most recent data for all measures is comparable and complete</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Selected potentially preventable hospitalisations – most recent data for at least one measure is comparable and complete</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A description of the comparability and completeness is provided under the Indicator results tab for each measure.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div role="tabpanel" class="hidden printonly" aria-hidden="true" aria-labelledby="indicator-results"><h3 class="printonly mt-8 mb-4 font-bold text-4xl text-blue-dark print-tab-title">Indicator Results</h3><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">This section presents an overview of &#x27;Primary and community health&#x27; performance indicator results. Different delivery contexts, locations and types of clients can affect the equity, effectiveness and efficiency of primary and community health services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Information to assist the interpretation of this data can be found with the indicators below and all data (footnotes and data sources) is available for download above as an excel spreadsheet and as a CSV dataset. Data tables are identified by a ‘10A’ prefix (for example, table 10A.1).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Specific data used in figures can be downloaded by clicking in the figure area, navigating to the bottom of the visualisation to the grey toolbar, clicking on the &#x27;Download&#x27; icon and selecting &#x27;Data&#x27; from the menu. Selecting &#x27;PDF&#x27; or &#x27;Powerpoint&#x27; from the &#x27;Download&#x27; menu will download a static view of the performance indicator results.</p></div></div><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="flex justify-end mb-2"><button class="break-words font-semibold font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 text-button-s px-1 py-1 rounded-md bg-white inline-flex text-blue-dark border-blue-mid hover:text-blue-dark active:text-blue-dark focus:text-blue-dark disabled:text-grey [&amp;&gt;i]:text-blue-dark [&amp;&gt;i]:shrink-0 hover:bg-grey-dark/5 noprint-inline">Expand All</button></div><div class="bg-white" data-block="true" data-orientation="vertical"><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_gujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="1-availability-of-primary-healthcare-services" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">1. Availability of primary healthcare services</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_gujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Availability of primary healthcare services’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to provide access to primary healthcare services in an equitable manner.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Availability of primary healthcare services’ is defined by four measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Access to approved suppliers of PBS medicines, defined as the ABS Census or Estimated Resident Population divided by the number of approved suppliers of PBS medicines, by metropolitan/rural and remote location under the Modified Monash Model (MMM) classification</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">GPs by region, defined as the number of FTE GPs per 100,000 people, by region</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">GPs by sex, defined as the number of FTE GPs per 100,000 people, by sex</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Public dentists by region, defined as the number of FTE public dentists per 100,000 people (based on clinical hours worked in the public sector), by region.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A similar rate across regions is desirable as it indicates equity of access by location. A similar rate by sex is desirable as it means patients who prefer to visit GPs of their own sex are more likely to have their preference met. A low rate of GPs of either sex could be associated with increased waiting times to see a GP for patients who prefer to visit GPs of their own sex.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measures on GPs by region and sex, and public dentists by region do not provide information on whether people are accessing services or whether the services are appropriate for the needs of the people receiving them.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Nationally at 30 June 2025, there were 4,528 people per approved PBS provider in metropolitan areas and 3,439 people per approved PBS supplier in rural and remote areas (figure 10.2a). The number of people per approved PBS provider in metropolitan and rural and remote areas is the highest over the available time series (table 10A.18). In 2025 the MMM was updated to align with the latest available census data (2021). Data is also available for pharmacy suppliers only (table 10A.18) and by MMM area (table 10A.17).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measures 2–3: Nationally in 2024, the number of FTE GPs per 100,000 people was greater in major cities (111.3 GPs per 100,000 people) compared to outer regional, remote and very remote areas (89.2 GPs per 100,000 people) (figure 10.2b). Nationally in 2024, there were 94.1 FTE female GPs per 100,000 females and 126.5 FTE male GPs per 100,000 males (tables 10A.20–21).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 4: Nationally in 2024, the rate of FTE public dentists per 100,000 people was higher in remote and very remote areas (6.0 per 100,000 people) compared to other areas (between 4.8–⁠5.6 per 100,000 people) (figure 10.2c). Data on FTE public dentists and allied dental practitioners are presented in table 10A.22. </p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_hejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="2-health-checks-for-aboriginal-and-torres-strait-islander-people" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">2. Health checks for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_hejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_1ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Health checks for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to provide access to primary and community healthcare in an equitable manner.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Health checks for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people’ is defined as the number of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people who had an Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander-specific Medicare health check, by assessment location divided by the number of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people. The measure is presented as a rate per 1,000 people.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">An increase over time in the proportion of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people who received a health check is desirable as it indicates improved access to these services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">This indicator provides no information about health checks provided outside Medicare (predominantly used by Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people in remote and very remote areas). Accordingly, this indicator understates the proportion of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people who received health check services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Nationally in 2024-25, 254.6 per 1,000 Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people had an Indigenous-specific health check, an increase from 252.4 in 2023-24 and close to the peak of 255.8 per 1,000 people in 2018-19 (figure 10.3). Nationally in 2024-25, Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people mainly received Indigenous-specific health checks at health facilities or residential aged care (251.8 per 1,000 people), with 3.0 per 1,000 people obtaining checks via telehealth (table 10A.23).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data on health checks or assessments, by location of assessment is reported in table 10A.24. Data by age group is reported in table 10A.25.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_hujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="3-affordability-of-primary-healthcare-services" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">3. Affordability of primary healthcare services</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_hujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_1ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Affordability of primary healthcare services’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to provide primary healthcare services that are affordable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Affordability of primary healthcare’ is defined by two measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">People delaying or not seeing a GP due to cost, defined as the proportion of people who delayed seeing or did not see a GP when needed at any time in the previous 12 months due to cost.</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">People delaying or not filling prescription medication due to cost, defined as the proportion of people who delayed filling or did not fill a prescription when needed at any time in the previous 12 months due to cost.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A low or decreasing proportion of people deferring visits to GPs or filling prescription medication due to cost indicates more widely affordable access to GPs and medicines and is considered desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data is sourced from the ABS Patient Experience Survey (PEx) of people aged 15 years or over. The PEx does not include people living in discrete Indigenous communities, which affects the representativeness of the Northern Territory results. Approximately 20% of the resident population of the Northern Territory live in discrete Indigenous communities as of 2020-21.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Nationally in 2024-25, 7.7% of respondents who needed to see a GP reported that they delayed or did not see a GP in the last 12 months due to cost, a decrease from 2023-24 (8.8%) and a departure from year-on-year increases since 2020-21 when the proportion was 2.4% (figure 10.4a). </p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Contextual information on bulk-billing and out-of-pocket costs are provided to assist interpretation of this indicator. Bulk billing information is available for both patients and services (one patient may have more than one service in a given year).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Nationally in 2024-25, 77.9% of non-referred GP services and 28.2% of specialist services were bulk billed. Information on bulk billing rates for non-referred GP services and specialist services by jurisdiction, region and age are available in tables 10A.27–30. Nationally in 2024-25, 49.1% of patients were fully bulk billed, reversing a downward trend since a peak of 67.6% in 2020-21 (table 10A.31). Nationally in 2024-25, out-of-pocket costs were highest for specialists ($126), followed by allied health services ($71) and non-referred GPs ($49) (table 10A.32).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 2: Nationally in 2024-25, 7.5% of respondents who needed a prescription for medication reported that they delayed filling or did not fill a prescription in the last 12 months due to cost, a decrease from 2023-24 (8.0%) and a departure from year-on-year increases since 2020-21 when the proportion was 4.4% (figure 10.4b). </p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_iejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="4-timeliness-of-primary-healthcare-services" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">4. Timeliness of primary healthcare services</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_iejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_2ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Timeliness of primary healthcare services’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to ensure primary healthcare services are provided in a timely manner.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Timeliness of primary healthcare services’ is defined by two measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Public dentistry waiting times, defined as the number of days waited at the 50th (median) and 90th percentiles between being placed on a selected public dentistry waiting list and either being offered dental care or receiving dental care.</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">GP waiting times for urgent medical care, defined as the proportion of people who, in the previous 12 months, saw a GP for urgent medical care within specified times from making the appointment. Specified waiting times are less than four hours, four to less than 24 hours, 24 hours or more.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A shorter time waited to see a dental professional indicates more timely access to public dental services. A high or increasing proportion of people who saw a GP within four hours for urgent medical care indicates more timely access to GPs.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Public dental waiting times only include records on persons eligible for public dental services who were aged 18 years or over. It excludes those on jurisdictional priority client schemes and those who access the service but pay full price. Data is reported by Indigenous status, remoteness area of residence, and Socio-Economic Indexes for Areas (SEIFA) of residence.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data is sourced from the ABS PEx of people aged 15 years or over. The PEx does not include people living in discrete Indigenous communities, which affects the representativeness of the Northern Territory results. Approximately 20% of the estimated resident population of the Northern Territory live in discrete Indigenous communities as of 2020-21.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Data for the time waited at the 50th and 90th percentiles by people on selected public dental waiting lists are presented for states and territories (figure 10.5a). </p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div class="rogs-comment"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measure 2: Nationally in 2024-25, for people who saw a GP for urgent care:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">41.6% waited less than four hours, similar to 2023-24 (41.7%) although a 22.0 percentage point decrease from 63.6% in 2015-16</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">11.1% waited from four to less than 24 hours, a decrease on 2023-24 (12.5%) and a slight decrease from 2015-16 (11.9%)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">47.0% waited for 24 hours or more, a slight increase on 2023-24 (46.0%) although a 22.5 percentage point increase from 24.5% in 2015-16 (figure 10.5b).</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Overall, nationally in 2024-25, 26.0% of people who saw a GP for their own health waited longer than they felt was acceptable to get an appointment, a decrease on 2023-24 (28.0%), although a 9.1 percentage point increase across the time series (18.9% in 2015-16) (table 10A.44). </p></div></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_iujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="5-potentially-avoidable-presentations-to-emergency-departments" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">5. Potentially avoidable presentations to emergency departments</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_iujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_2ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Potentially avoidable presentations to emergency departments’ (also known as ‘GP-type presentations’) is an indicator of governments’ objective for primary and community healthcare to be accessible.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Potentially avoidable presentations to emergency departments (interim measure) are defined as the number of selected ‘GP‑type presentations’ to emergency departments, where selected GP‑type presentations are emergency presentations:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">allocated to triage category 4 (semi‑urgent) or 5 (non‑urgent)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">not arriving by ambulance, with police or corrections</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">not admitted or referred to another hospital and</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">who did not die.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Potentially avoidable presentations to emergency departments are presentations for conditions that could be appropriately managed in the primary and community health sector. In some cases, this can be determined only retrospectively and presentation to an emergency department is appropriate. Factors contributing to GP‑type presentations at emergency departments include perceived or actual lack of access to GP services, the proximity of emergency departments and trust in emergency department staff.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Once a suitable denominator for this measure is agreed, a low or decreasing rate or proportion of potentially avoidable presentations to emergency departments can indicate better access to primary and community health care. Currently, the <em>number</em> of potentially avoidable presentations to emergency departments are reported for this indicator. In future, this indicator will be reported as a <em>proportion</em> (for example, the number of potentially avoidable GP-type presentations to emergency departments, as a proportion of all presentations to emergency departments), subject to the identification of a suitable denominator.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Nationally in 2024-25, there were around 2.7 million GP-type presentations to public hospital emergency departments, the lowest in the available time series (table 10.1). Results varied across jurisdictions. </p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_jejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="6-developmental-health-checks" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">6. Developmental health checks</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_jejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_3ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Developmental health checks’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to ensure that services are appropriate and responsive to the needs of children.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Developmental health checks’ are defined as the proportion of preschool-aged children who received a developmental health assessment.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A high or increasing proportion of preschool-aged children receiving developmental health checks is desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">This indicator is currently under development for reporting in the future.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_jujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="7-chronic-disease-management" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">7. Chronic disease management</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_jujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_3ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Chronic disease management’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to ensure that primary and community health services are appropriate and responsive to meet the needs of individuals throughout their lifespan.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Chronic disease management’ is defined by two measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Rate of chronic disease care services (under Team Care Arrangements (TCAs) and GP Management Plans (GPMPs)).</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Management of asthma, defined as the proportion of people with asthma who have a written asthma action plan.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measure 1 focuses on patients who had a GPMP prepared or a TCA co-ordination service. These are the two most commonly used Medicare items for people with a chronic or terminal medical condition. Claiming patterns of chronic disease management services vary by demographic characteristics, jurisdiction, remoteness and socio-economic areas. Medicare data does not give a comprehensive picture of actual service delivery and data does not include chronic disease care services provided under other arrangements that do not attract a Medicare subsidy.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">For measure 2, data is sourced from the ABS National Health Survey. The survey does not include people living in very remote areas and discrete Indigenous communities, which affects the representativeness of the Northern Territory results for the asthma measure. Approximately 20% of the estimated resident population of the Northern Territory live in very remote areas and discrete Indigenous communities as of 2020-21.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A high or increasing proportion for each measure is desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Nationally in 2024-25, the rate of patients claiming for GPMP services was 133.6 per 1,000 people, the highest rate over the time series. The rate of patients claiming for TCAs was 116.1 per 1,000 people, also the highest rate for the same period (Figure 10.6a).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 2: Nationally in 2022, the age-standardised proportion of people with asthma reporting that they have a written asthma action plan was 35.4% compared to 32.3% in 2017-18 (figure 10.6b). In all jurisdictions, the proportion was higher for children aged 0–14 years than for other age groups (nationally, 71.5%) (table 10A.46).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Biomedical data on the management of diabetes is available in earlier reports.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_kejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="8-immunisation-coverage" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">8. Immunisation coverage</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_kejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_4ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Immunisation coverage’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to ensure primary and community health services are appropriate and responsive to meet the needs of individuals throughout their life span and communities.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Immunisation coverage’ is defined by four measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Proportion of children aged 12 months to less than 15 months who are fully immunised (at this age, against diphtheria, tetanus, pertussis (whooping cough), polio, hepatitis B, <em>Haemophilus influenzae</em> type b and pneumococcal).</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Proportion of children aged 24 months to less than 27 months who are fully immunised (at this age, against diphtheria, tetanus, pertussis (whooping cough), polio, <em>Haemophilus influenzae</em> type b, hepatitis B, measles, mumps and rubella, meningococcal C, varicella and pneumococcal).</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Proportion of children aged 60 months to less than 63 months who are fully immunised (at this age, against diphtheria, tetanus, pertussis (whooping cough) and polio).</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Proportion of people aged 65 years or over who have been vaccinated against seasonal influenza.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">High or increasing proportions of immunisation coverage are desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measures 1–3: Nationally in 2024-25, the proportion of children fully immunised was: 91.6% for children aged 12 to less than 15 months; 89.8% for children aged 24 to less than 27 months; and 93.3% for children aged 60 to less than 63 months (figure 10.7a). For all age cohorts, the proportions of children fully immunised in 2024-25 were amongst the lowest over the reported time series. </p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Contextual data on vaccinations supplied to children aged under 7 years, by type of provider is reported in table 10A.47.</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 4: Nationally in 2025, 58.4% of people aged 65 years or over were vaccinated against seasonal influenza, with the proportion slightly higher for non-Indigenous people (58.4%) compared to Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people (56.6%) (figure 10.7b).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_kujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="9-cancer-screening" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">9. Cancer screening</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_kujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_4ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Cancer screening’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to ensure primary and community health services are appropriate and responsive to meet the needs of individuals throughout their life span and communities.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Cancer screening’ is defined by three measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Participation in breast cancer screening, defined as the proportion of women aged 50–74 years who were screened in the BreastScreen Australia Program over a 24‑month period.</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Participation in cervical screening, defined as the number of participants aged 25–74 years screened in a five-and-a-half-year period as a percentage of eligible females in the population.</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Participation in bowel cancer screening, defined as people aged 50–74 years who were invited to participate in the National Bowel Cancer Screening Program over a 24‑month period and returned a completed test kit within six months of the end of that period, divided by the number of invitations issued minus those people who opted out or suspended without completing their screening test.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">In November 2025, the Australian, state and territory governments agreed to a new definition of cervical screening participation.
