More details can be found at <a href="https://www.aihw.gov.au/reports/cancer-screening/ncsp-monitoring-report-2025" class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 transition-[text-decoration-offset, brightness] disabled:pointer-events-none border underline-offset-[25%] decoration-[5%] hover:underline-offset-[35%] duration-200 rounded-xl bg-transparent p-0 font-normal text-blue-dark border-transparent hover:text-blue-mid hover:underline active:bg-grey-200 text-base" id="httpswwwaihwgovaureportscancer-screeningncsp-monitoring-report-2025">https://www.aihw.gov.au/reports/cancer-screening/ncsp-monitoring-report-2025<i class="inline-block align-middle h-[1em] w-[1em] text-blue-mid shrink-0 ml-1"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M18 13V19C18 19.5304 17.7893 20.0391 17.4142 20.4142C17.0391 20.7893 16.5304 21 16 21H5C4.46957 21 3.96086 20.7893 3.58579 20.4142C3.21071 20.0391 3 19.5304 3 19V8C3 7.46957 3.21071 6.96086 3.58579 6.58579C3.96086 6.21071 4.46957 6 5 6H11" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path><path d="M15 3H21V9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path><path d="M10 14L21 3" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></a>. </p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">High or increasing screening participation rates are desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: The national age-standardised BreastScreen participation rate for women aged 50–74 years for 2023–2024 was 51.9% (figure 10.8a), an increase from 2022–2023 (51.2%). While screening rates have increased, they have not returned to pre-pandemic levels (54.2% in 2018-2019). For 2023–2024, the participation rate for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander women aged 50–74 years was 38.2%, also an increase from 2022–2023 (37.1%). However, this was lower than the screening rates for all women; a trend consistent across the available time series (table 10A.51).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 2: For the five-and-a-half-year period between 1 January 2020 and 30 June 2025, the national age-standardised participation rate in cervical cancer screening for participants aged 25–74 years was 78.1% (table 10.3).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 3: For 2023–2024, the national participation rate for people aged 50–74 years in bowel cancer screening was 42.0%, an increase from 41.7% in 2022–2023 (figure 10.8b).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_lejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="gp-accred" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">10. General practice accreditation</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_lejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_5ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘General practice accreditation’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to provide general practice services that meet the expected standards for safety and quality.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Accreditation’ is defined as general practices that have become accredited against the Royal Australian College of General Practitioners (RACGP) Standards under the National General Practice Accreditation (NGPA) Scheme and is measured as:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the number of general practices accredited, as a proportion of all general practices with completed accreditation assessments in the same calendar year</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the number of general practices accredited during the calendar year that required remedial actions to achieve accreditation, as a proportion of all general practices accredited during the same calendar year.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Accreditation is a voluntary process conducted by trained independent reviewers. It assesses general practices against a set of 17 standards developed by the RACGP (RACGP 2023). Accreditation is valid for three years after which reaccreditation is required. The core standards are:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">communication and patient participation</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">rights and needs of patients</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">practice governance and management</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">health promotion and preventative activities</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">clinical management of health issues</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">information management</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">content of patient health records</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">education and training of non-clinical staff.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A high or increasing rate of accreditation is desirable. Accreditation against the RACGP Standards can indicate an improvement in the capability of a general practice to deliver high quality services (RACGP 2023). However, general practices without accreditation may deliver services of equally high quality. For a particular general practice, the decision to seek accreditation might be influenced by perceived costs and benefits unrelated to its quality standards.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1–2: Nationally in 2024, 99% of all general practices assessed for accreditation were accredited (table 10A.54). The proportion of general practices requiring remedial actions to achieve accreditation was lower in 2024 (78%) than in 2023 (82%) (table 10.4).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_lujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="11-patient-satisfaction" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">11. Patient satisfaction</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_lujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_5ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Patient satisfaction’ is an indicator of governments’ objective that primary and community health services are high quality.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Patient satisfaction’ is defined as the quality of care as perceived by the patient. It is measured as patient experience of aspects of care that are key factors in patient outcomes and can be readily modified.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Two measures of patient experience of communication with health professionals – a key aspect of care – are reported:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the proportion of people who needed to and saw a GP in the previous 12 months who reported the GP always or often:<ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">listened carefully to them</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">showed respect</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">spent enough time with them</li></ul></li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the proportion of people who needed to and saw a dental professional in the previous 12 months who reported the dental professional always or often:<ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">listened carefully to them</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">showed respect</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">spent enough time with them.</li></ul></li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">High or increasing proportions can indicate improved satisfaction with the quality of care from the patient’s perspective.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data is sourced from the ABS PEx of people aged 15 years or over. The PEx does not include people living in discrete Indigenous communities, which affects the representativeness of the Northern Territory results. Approximately 20% of the resident population of the Northern Territory live in discrete Indigenous communities as of 2020-21.</p><div class="rogs-comment"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measure 1: Nationally in 2024-25, the majority of respondents who needed to and saw a GP reported that the GP always or often: </p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">listened carefully (90.6%)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">showed respect (94.2%)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">spent enough time with them (88.9%) (figure 10.9a; table 10A.55).</li></ul></div></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div class="rogs-comment"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measure 2: Nationally in 2024-25, the majority of respondents who needed to and saw a dental professional reported that the dental professional always or often: </p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">listened carefully (95.7%)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">showed respect (96.9%)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">spent enough time with them (96.8%) (figure 10.9b; table 10A.57).</li></ul></div></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data by remoteness for measures 1 and 2 are presented in tables 10A.55–58.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_mejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="12-continuity-of-care" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">12. Continuity of care</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_mejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_6ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Continuity of care’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to ensure that services are well coordinated when more than one service type and/or ongoing service provision is required.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Continuity of care’ is defined by three measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the proportion of GP management plans and team care assessment plans that have been reviewed in the last 12 months</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the proportion of people who saw three or more health professionals in the last 12 months for the same condition and who reported that a health professional helped coordinate their care and that this coordination of care helped to a large extent</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the proportion of people who saw three or more health professionals in the last 12 months for the same condition and who reported that issues were caused by a lack of communication between the health professionals.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">For the first measure, proportions are calculated by dividing the number of Medicare subsidised GP management plans and team care assessment plans reviewed (Medicare item no. 732), by the total number of Medicare subsidised GP management plans (Medicare item no. 721) and team care assessment plans (Medicare item no. 723), multiplied by 100.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A high or increasing proportion of GP management and team care assessment plans reviewed is desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data for the second and third measures is sourced from the ABS PEx of people aged 15 years or over. The PEx does not include people living in discrete Indigenous communities, which affects the representativeness of the Northern Territory results for both measures. Approximately 20% of the resident population of the Northern Territory live in discrete Indigenous communities as of 2020-21.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">For the second measure, a high or increasing proportion of patients who saw three or more different health professionals in the past 12 months for the same condition and who reported that a health professional helped coordinate their care and that this coordination of care helped to a large extent is desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">For the third measure, a low or decreasing proportion of patients who saw three or more different health professionals in the past 12 months for the same condition and who reported that issues were caused by a lack of communication between health professionals is desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Nationally in 2024-25, 68.8% of Medicare subsidised GP management plans and team care assessment plans were reviewed. This is the lowest proportion over the nine-year time series and reflects a continuing decrease since the peak in 2018-19 (74.1%) (figure 10.10a). </p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div class="rogs-comment"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measures 2–3: Nationally in 2024-25, the proportion of people who saw three or more health professionals in the last 12 months for the same condition and who reported that: </p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">a health professional helped coordinate their care and that this coordination of care helped to a large extent was 68.5% (figure 10.10b)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">issues were caused by a lack of communication between the health professionals was 16.3% (figure 10.10c). </li></ul></div></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_mujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="work-sus" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">13. Workforce sustainability</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_mujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_6ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Workforce sustainability’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to provide sustainable primary and community healthcare services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Workforce sustainability’ is defined by two measures:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the proportion of FTE GPs in 10-year age brackets</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">the attrition rate of FTE GPs who exit the workforce as a proportion of the number of FTE GPs by age bracket.</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A high or increasing percentage of the workforce that are new entrants and/or low or decreasing proportions of the workforce that are close to retirement is desirable. A low or decreasing rate of workforce attrition is desirable.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Health workforce sustainability relates to the capacity of the health workforce to meet current and projected future service demand. These measures are not a substitute for comprehensive workforce analysis that considers training, migration, emerging work patterns and future demand. However, they can indicate the need for further workforce planning for primary and community health services.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The attrition rate is measured as the proportion of GPs who were in scope in one calendar year, but not in scope in the following calendar year. In scope is defined as Primary Care GPs, being GPs working in the treatment of non‑admitted patients in the community. GPs who ‘exited’ (i.e. were no longer in scope) in 2022 might still be in the medical workforce and practicing as a GP but are classified as an exit as they are no longer Primary Care GPs.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data by sex for each measure is reported in tables 10A.62<span style="-webkit-text-stroke-width:0px;background-color:rgb(255, 255, 255);color:rgb(0, 0, 0);display:inline !important;float:none;font-family:__Open_Sans_908882, __Open_Sans_Fallback_908882, ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif, &quot;Apple Color Emoji&quot;, &quot;Segoe UI Emoji&quot;, &quot;Segoe UI Symbol&quot;, &quot;Noto Color Emoji&quot;;font-size:19px;font-style:normal;font-variant-caps:normal;font-variant-ligatures:normal;font-weight:400;letter-spacing:normal;orphans:2;text-align:start;text-decoration-color:initial;text-decoration-style:initial;text-decoration-thickness:initial;text-indent:0px;text-transform:none;white-space:normal;widows:2;word-spacing:0px">–</span>64.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Nationally in 2024, 26.4% of FTE GPs were aged 60 years or over, compared to 1.5% who were less than 30 years of age (figure 10.11). For all GPs, the proportion of FTE female and male GPs aged under 50 years is nearly equal, at 23.6% and 23.7% respectively. This represents the smallest difference recorded in the available time series. Although the gap is larger among GPs aged 50 years or over, with 19.4% female and 33.3% male, this also marks the smallest difference observed for this age group over the same period.</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 2: Nationally in 2024, the proportion of GPs who exited the GP workforce was 1.2%, with the proportion highest for people aged 60 years or over (2.5%) (table 10.5). The proportion of female GPs aged under 30 years who exited the workforce remains steady at 1.6%, while the proportion of male GPs aged under 30 years who exited the workforce (0.9%) is the lowest in the time series (table 10A.63).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_nejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="14-cost-to-government-of-general-practice-per-person" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">14. Cost to government of general practice per person</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_nejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_7ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Cost to government of general practice per person’ is an indicator of governments’ objective to provide primary and community health services in an efficient manner.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Cost to government of general practice per person’ is defined as the cost to government of general practice per person in the population.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">This indicator should be interpreted with care. A low or decreasing cost per person can indicate higher efficiency, provided services are equally or more effective. It can also reflect service substitution between primary healthcare and hospital or specialist services – potentially at greater expense.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Cost to government of general practice does not capture the costs of salaried GP service delivery models, used particularly in rural or remote areas. Salaried GP service models involve the delivery of primary healthcare services by salaried GPs in community health settings, emergency departments, and Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander primary healthcare services. Therefore, costs are understated for jurisdictions where a larger proportion of the population live in rural and remote areas.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Nationally in 2024-25, total expenditure per person on general practice was $489 per person, an increase in real terms from $469 in 2023-24 (figure 10.12).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_nujkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="15-notifications-of-selected-childhood-diseases" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">15. Notifications of selected childhood diseases</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_nujkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_7ujkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Notifications of selected childhood diseases’ is an indicator of governments’ objective for primary and community health services to promote health and prevent illness.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Notifications of selected childhood diseases’ is defined as the number of notifications of measles, pertussis and invasive <em>Haemophilus influenzae</em> type b reported to the National Notifiable Diseases Surveillance System by state and territory health authorities for children aged 0–14 years, per 100,000 children in that age group.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">A low or reducing notification rate for the selected diseases indicates that the immunisation program is more effective.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Measles, pertussis (whooping cough) and invasive <em>Haemophilus influenzae</em> type b are nationally notifiable vaccine preventable diseases, and notification to the relevant state or territory authority is required on diagnosis.</p><div class="rogs-comment"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Nationally in 2024-25, the rate of notifications for children aged 0–14 years was:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">0.1 per 100,000 children for Haemophilus influenzae type b, steady since 2022-23</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">0.5 per 100,000 children for measles, equivalent to 2023-24 and consistent with the average annual rate of notifications over the past 10 years</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">792.1 per 100,000 children for pertussis (whooping cough), a significant increase since 2023-24 (201.2 per 100,000 children) (figure 10.13; table 10A.64).</li></ul></div></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">In 2024-25, Australia experienced a surge in whooping cough cases, the highest since monitoring started in 1991 (DHDA 2025c).</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_oejkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="16-selected-potentially-preventable-hospitalisations" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">16. Selected potentially preventable hospitalisations</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_oejkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_8ejkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Selected potentially preventable hospitalisations’ is an indicator of governments’ objective for primary and community health services to promote health, prevent illness and to support people to manage their health issues in the community.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">‘Selected potentially preventable hospitalisations’ is defined as hospital admissions that may be avoided by effective management of illness and injury in the primary and community healthcare sector or, in some cases, by preventing illness and injury altogether.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Two measures of selected potentially preventable hospitalisations are reported by state or territory of residence:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Potentially preventable hospitalisations for selected vaccine preventable, acute and chronic conditions.</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Potentially preventable hospitalisations for diabetes (Type 2 diabetes mellitus as principal diagnosis).</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Low or decreasing separation rates for selected potentially preventable hospitalisations can indicate more effective management of selected conditions in the primary and community healthcare sector and/or more effective preventative programs. However, hospitalisation rates are also influenced by factors outside the control of the primary and community healthcare sector. For example, the underlying prevalence of conditions, patient compliance with management and older people’s access to aged care services and other support.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data on potentially preventable hospitalisations for selected vaccine preventable, acute and chronic conditions as a rate per 1,000 people is reported in tables 10A.67–71.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 1: Nationally in 2023-24, the age-standardised hospital separation rate for selected vaccine preventable, acute and chronic conditions was 26.2 per 1,000 people, an increase on 2022-23 (table 10.6). The rate for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people was 68.9 per 1,000 people (table 10A.66). The rate for people living in very remote areas (67.7 per 1,000 people) was almost 3 times greater than people living in major cities (24.3%) (tables 10A.67).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The age-standardised separation rate for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people (excluding separations for diabetes complications as an additional diagnosis) was 2.5 times the rate for all Australians (table 10A.72).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The most serious complication of Type 2 diabetes most commonly leading to hospitalisation in 2023-24 was circulatory complications, with an age-standardised rate of 17.4 per 100,000 people (table 10A.73). The proportion of Type 2 diabetes complications leading to hospitalisation by complication type is reported in table 10A.74.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Serious circulatory complications of diabetes can necessitate lower limb amputation. In 2023-24, there were 21.2 age-standardised hospital separations per 100,000 people for lower limb amputations where Type 2 diabetes mellitus was a principal or additional diagnosis (table 10A.75).</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words rogs-comment">Measure 2: Nationally in 2023-24, the age-standardised hospital separation rate for diabetes was 138.8 separations per 100,000 people (table 10.7).</p></div></div><!--$!--><template data-dgst="BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING"></template><!--/$--></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div role="tabpanel" class="hidden printonly" aria-hidden="true" aria-labelledby="aboriginal-and-torres-strait-islander-data"><h3 class="printonly mt-8 mb-4 font-bold text-4xl text-blue-dark print-tab-title">Aboriginal And Torres Strait Islander Data</h3><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Data for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people in this section is available in the data tables listed below. Further supporting information can be found in the Indicator results tab and data tables.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Further information on the historical and ongoing context for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people is available on the <a href="https://www.pc.gov.au/closing-the-gap-data/dashboard/outcome-area/long-and-healthy-lives#context-button" class="transition-colors break-words inline font-sans items-center justify-center gap-2 [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="flex justify-end mb-2"><button class="break-words font-semibold font-sans items-center px-1 py-1 rounded-md bg-white inline-flex text-blue-dark border-blue-mid hover:text-blue-dark active:text-blue-dark focus:text-blue-dark disabled:text-grey [&amp;&gt;i]:text-blue-dark data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 last:mb-0" data-accordion-item="true"><h3 data-orientation="vertical" data-state="closed" class="flex"><button type="button" aria-controls="radix-_R_1flkt5feiv5u5b_" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="key-terms" class="flex flex-1 items-center bg-blue-light p-2.5 text-left transition-all [&amp;[data-state=closed]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-0 [&amp;[data-state=open]&gt;div&gt;[data-accordion-icon]]:rotate-180" data-radix-collection-item=""><div><div class="font-sans text-heading-s font-normal">Key terms</div></div><div class="ml-auto"></div><div class="ml-8 print:hidden"><i class="inline-block align-middle size-4 text-black transition-transform" data-accordion-icon="true"><svg fill="none" width="100%" height="100%" viewBox="0 0 24 24" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><path d="M6 9L12 15L18 9" fill-rule="evenodd" clip-rule="evenodd" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"></path></svg></i></div></button></h3><div data-state="closed" id="radix-_R_1flkt5feiv5u5b_" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-_R_flkt5feiv5u5b_" data-orientation="vertical" class="print:!block data-[state=closed]:hidden" style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-2.5 pt-4 pb-8"><div id="contentBlocks" class="w-full [&amp;&gt;*]:mb-14 [&amp;&gt;*:last-child]:mb-0"><div class="mx-auto container p-0 md:px-0 2xl:px-0"><div class="[&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;div&gt;:last-child]:mb-0 max-w-[996px]"><div class="w-full overflow-auto"><table class="mt-8 mb-4 markup-table w-full border-separate border-spacing-[2px] text-sm [&amp;_td&gt;p:last-child]:mb-0 [&amp;_td]:p-2 [&amp;_td]:align-baseline md:[&amp;_td]:p-3.5 [&amp;_th&gt;p]:mb-0 [&amp;_th]:p-2 [&amp;_th]:text-left md:[&amp;_th]:p-3.5 [&amp;_td]:bg-white [&amp;_th]:text-grey-200" data-block="true"><thead><tr><th class="wide20" scope="col" id="table29468r1c1">Terms</th><th scope="col" id="table29468r1c2">Definition</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r2c1">Age-standardised</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r2c1">Removing the effect of different age distributions (across jurisdictions or over time) when making comparisons, by weighting the age‑specific rates for each jurisdiction by the national age distribution.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r3c1">Allied health practitioner</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r3c1">Trained professionals with university qualifications (other than qualified medical or nursing staff) who are engaged in duties of a diagnostic, professional or technical nature. This category includes physiotherapists, psychologists, social workers, occupational therapists, Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander health practitioners, and other diagnostic and health professionals.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r4c1">Annual cycle of care for people with diabetes mellitus within general practice</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r4c1"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The annual cycle of care comprises the components of care, delivered over the course of a year, that are minimum requirements for the appropriate management of diabetes in general practice based on RACGP guidelines.</p><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">Medicare items can be claimed on completion of the annual cycle of care according to Medicare requirements for management, which are based on but not identical to the RACGP guidelines.</p></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r5c1">Asthma action plan</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r5c1">The National Asthma Council Australia recommends people with asthma should have their own individual written action plan that includes instructions on what to do when asthma symptoms worsen (National Asthma Council 2022).</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r7c1">Cervical Screening Test</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r7c1">A Cervical Screening Test consists of a human papillomavirus (HPV) test with partial genotyping and, if the HPV test detects oncogenic HPV, liquid-based cytology.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r8c1">Closed treatment episode</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r8c1">A closed treatment episode is a period of contact between a client and an alcohol and other drug treatment agency. It has defined dates of commencement and cessation, during which the principal drug of concern, treatment delivery setting and main treatment type did not change. Reasons for cessation of a treatment episode include treatment completion, and client non‑participation in treatment for three months or more. Clients may have more than one closed treatment episode in a data collection period.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r9c1">Community health services</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r9c1">Health services for individuals and groups delivered in a community setting, rather than in hospitals or private facilities.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r12c1">Consultations</th><td headers="table29468r12c1 table29468r1c2">Periods of service provided by GPs. Professional attendance by a GP can include any of the following that are clinically relevant: taking a patient history; performing a clinical examination; arranging any necessary investigation; implementing a management plan; and providing appropriate preventive health care.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r13c1">Cost to government of general practice per person</th><td headers="table29468r13c1 table29468r1c2">Cost to the Australian Government of total non‑referred attendances by non‑specialist medical practitioners per person.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r14c1">General practice</th><td headers="table29468r14c1 table29468r1c2">The organisational structure with one or more GPs and other staff such as practice nurses. A general practice provides and supervises healthcare for a ‘population’ of patients and may include services for specific populations, such as women’s health or Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander health.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r15c1">General practitioner (GP)</th><td headers="table29468r15c1 table29468r1c2"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">To be recognised as a specialist general practitioner for the purposes of Medicare, medical practitioners must either:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">hold specialist registration as a general practitioner with the Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency (Ahpra)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">participate in an approved workforce or training program (commonly known as 3GA programs).</li></ul><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">To be registered as a specialist general practitioner by the Ahpra, general practitioners must hold fellowship of the Royal Australian College of General Practitioners (RACGP) or the Australian College of Rural and Remote Medicine (ACRRM). Medical practitioners who were on the vocational register on 16 June 2021 maintain their access to general practice items in the Medicare Benefits Schedule.</p></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r16c1">General practitioner full time equivalent (GP FTE)</th><td headers="table29468r16c1 table29468r1c2">GP FTE is a workforce specific method to estimate the workload of GPs. The method calculates a GP’s workload based on Medicare services claimed as well as patient and doctor factors that affect the duration of a consultation. One GP FTE represents a 40 hour week per week for 46 weeks of the year. For each Medicare provider, the measure attributes an estimate of the amount of time they have spent on their claims compared to what would be worked by a full-time GP, including billable time, non-billable time, and non-clinical time.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r17c1">General practitioner (GP) Headcount</th><td headers="table29468r17c1 table29468r1c2"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">GP Headcount is a workforce specific method of headcount for GPs working in Australia (number of GPs). The method uses elements from the Medicare data set to count when, where and by what type of practitioner GP services are being delivered. The number of GPs is based on the following aspects of Medicare data:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">Medicare items within GP&#x27;s scope of practice as agreed by Commonwealth Medical Advisors and GPs (Some Medicare items reviewed by Commonwealth Medical Advisors and GPs have been restricted in MM 1–2 to account for the difference in the scope of GP activity across metropolitan, regional, rural and remote areas.)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">A review of a GPs services over a whole year to determine their Main Derived Major Speciality</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">A unique identifier to enable distinct counts by speciality.</li></ul></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r18c1">GP‑type services</th><td headers="table29468r18c1 table29468r1c2">Non‑referred attendances by vocationally registered GPs, other medical practitioners and practice nurses.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r19c1"><em>Haemophilus influenzae</em> type b</th><td headers="table29468r19c1 table29468r1c2">A bacterium which causes bloodstream infection, meningitis, epiglottitis, and pneumonia (DHDA 2018).</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r20c1">Human papillomavirus (HPV) test</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r20c1">An HPV test uses a cervical or vaginal sample to test for the presence of oncogenic HPV types which could lead to the development of cervical cancer.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r21c1">ICD-10-AM</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r21c1">The International Statistical Classification of Diseases and Related Health Problems - 10th Revision - Australian modification (ICD-10-AM) is the current classification of diagnoses in Australia.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r22c1">Modified Monash Model</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r22c1">The Modified Monash Model (MMM) is a geographical classification that categorises areas in Australia into seven remoteness categories. The model measures remoteness and population size on a scale of MM 1 to MM 7. MM 1 is a major city and MM 7 is very remote.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r23c1">Non-referred attendances</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r23c1">GP services, emergency attendances after hours, other prolonged attendances, group therapy and acupuncture. All attendances for specialist services are excluded because these must be ‘referred’ to receive Services Australia Medicare reimbursement.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r24c1">Nationally notifiable disease</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r24c1">A communicable disease that is on the Communicable Diseases Network Australia’s endorsed list of diseases to be notified nationally (DHDA 2022). On diagnosis of these diseases, there is a requirement to notify the relevant State or Territory health authority.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r25c1">Other medical practitioner (OMP)</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r25c1">A medical practitioner other than a vocationally registered GP who has at least half of the schedule fee value of his/her Services Australia Medicare billing from non‑referred attendances. These practitioners are able to access only the lower A2 Services Australia Medicare rebate for general practice services they provide, unless the services are provided through certain Departmental incentive programs.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r26c1">Pap smear</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r26c1">A procedure used to detect pre-cancerous abnormalities of the cervix.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r27c1">PBS doctor’s bag</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r27c1">Emergency drug supplies provided without charge to prescribers for use in medical emergencies in the clinic or community at no charge to the patient.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r28c1">Per person benefits paid for GP ordered pathology</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r28c1">Total benefits paid under Services Australia Medicare for pathology tests requested by GPs, divided by the population.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r29c1">Per person benefits paid for GP referred diagnostic imaging</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r29c1">Total benefits paid for diagnostic imaging services performed on referral by GPs, divided by the population.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r30c1">Primary healthcare</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r30c1"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The primary and community healthcare sector includes services that:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">provide the first point of contact with the health system</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">have a particular focus on illness prevention or early intervention</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">are intended to maintain people’s independence and maximise their quality of life through care and support at home or in local community settings.</li></ul></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r31c1">Primary Health Networks</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r31c1">Primary Health Networks are a national network of independent primary health care organisations (replacing Medicare Locals from 1 July 2015) designed to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of medical services for patients at risk of poor health outcomes and improve care coordination, particularly for those with chronic and complex conditions.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r32c1">Prevalence</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r32c1">The number of cases of a disease present in a population at a given time (point prevalence) or during a given period (period prevalence).</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r33c1">Public health</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r33c1">The organised, social response to protect and promote health and to prevent illness, injury and disability. The starting point for identifying public health issues, problems and priorities, and for designing and implementing interventions, is the population as a whole or population subgroups. Public health is characterised by a focus on the health of the population (and particular at‑risk groups) and complements clinical provision of healthcare services.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r34c1">Recognised immunisation provider</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r34c1">A GP or an individual, or body, endorsed by the Commonwealth, a State or Territory to administer vaccines in Australia.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r35c1">Recognised specialist</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r35c1">A medical practitioner classified as a specialist by the Medical Board of Australia and on the Services Australia Medicare database earning at least half of his or her income from relevant specialist items in the schedule, having regard to the practitioner’s field of specialist recognition.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r36c1">Screening</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r36c1">The performance of a test or tests on apparently well people to detect a medical condition earlier than would otherwise be possible.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r37c1">Socio-Economic Indexes for Areas (SEIFA)</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r37c1">Socio-Economic Indexes for Areas (SEIFA) quintiles are based on the ABS Index of Relative Socio-Economic Disadvantage (IRSD), with quintile 1 being the most disadvantaged and quintile 5 being the least disadvantaged. Each SEIFA quintile represents approximately 20% of the national population, but does not necessarily represent 20% of the population in each state or territory.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" id="table29468r38c1">Triage category</th><td headers="table29468r1c2 table29468r38c1"><p class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal mb-4 break-words">The urgency of the patient’s need for medical and nursing care:</p><ul class="mb-4 mt-0 pl-8 [&amp;_ol]:mb-0 [&amp;_ul]:mb-0"><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">category 1 – resuscitation (immediate within seconds)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">category 2 – emergency (within 10 minutes)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">category 3 – urgent (within 30 minutes)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">category 4 – semi‑urgent (within 60 minutes)</li><li id="" class="font-sans text-paragraph-l font-normal m-0 mb-2 p-0 [&amp;&gt;ol]:mt-2 [&amp;&gt;p]:m-0 [&amp;&gt;ul]:mt-2">category 5 – non‑urgent (within 120 minutes).</li></ul></td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="group bg-transparent transition-colors mb-1 health\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003ePrimary and community health services aim to promote health, prevent illness and support people to manage their health issues in the community, by providing services that are:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"ee2f9d99553c66911d1458440e6b48770\"\u003etimely, affordable and accessible to all\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli services to meet these objectives in an equitable and efficient manner.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp class=\"hidenojs\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"accordian-expall toggle\"\u003eExpand all\u003c/a\u003e\u003ca class=\"accordian-collall toggle\"\u003eCollapse all\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cdiv health services are delivered by a range of health and allied health professionals across private, not‑for‑profit and government service settings. Definitions for common health terms are provided in the 'Explanatory material' tab.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003eGeneral practice\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eGeneral practice is a major provider of primary healthcare in Australia. Services include preventative care, the diagnosis and treatment of illness and injury, delivered directly and/or through referral to acute (hospital) or other healthcare services.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe Australian Government provides the majority of general practice income, through Services Australia – mainly as fee-for-service payments via the Medicare Benefits Schedule (Medicare) – and the Department of Veterans’ Affairs (DVA). Additional funding is provided to influence the supply, regional distribution and quality of general practice services, and support engagement of the health workforce in primary health care settings including residential aged care. This includes initiatives such as the Practice Incentives Program, the Workforce Incentive Program, the General Practice in Aged Care Incentive and Primary Health Networks (Services Australia 2025). State and territory governments also provide some funding for such programs, mainly to influence the availability of General Practitioners (GPs) in rural and remote areas. Other funding sources include insurance schemes (for example, workers compensation and traffic accident schemes) and patient contributions.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003ePharmaceutical services\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe Australian Government funds the Pharmaceutical Benefits Scheme (PBS), which subsidises the cost of many medicines in Australia. The PBS schedule sets a price for listed medicines and a maximum co-payment amount that people contribute towards the cost of these medicines. The Australian Government incurs the expense of any difference where the listed price exceeds the patient co-payment (whether for general or concessional patients).\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn 2022-23, around 69% of PBS prescriptions dispensed were above the co-payment threshold. Patients paid the co-payment and Australian Government subsidised the remainder. Around 31% were below the threshold, meaning the patient paid the full cost with no government subsidy (AIHW 2024a). Co-payments contribute to a patient’s safety net threshold that, once reached, provides eligibility to receive PBS medicines at a lower cost or free of charge (DHDA 2025a).\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe Repatriation Pharmaceutical Benefits Scheme (RPBS) provides subsidised pharmaceutical medicines, dressings and other items to war veterans and war widows. The RPBS is administered by the DVA.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003eDental services\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eAustralia has a mixed system of public and private dental services. State and territory governments deliver public dental services, which are primarily available to children and disadvantaged adults. The Australian Government works with state and territory governments to fund dental services. Since 2013, the Australian Government has increased funding for public dental services via National Partnership Agreements and Federal Funding Agreements with states and territories and the Child Dental Benefits Schedule. The private sector receives funding to provide some public dental services from the Australian Government through the DVA and the Child Dental Benefits Schedule, and from state and territory governments through dental voucher systems. The Australian Government is working with states and territories to develop the next ten-year \u003cem\u003eNational Oral Health Plan\u003c/em\u003e which will set out priorities to improve dental health across Australia (DHDA 2025b). Data on dental service expenditure for the years 2014-15 to 2023-24 is presented in table 10A.6.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003eAllied health services\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eAllied health services include, but are not limited to, physiotherapy, psychology, occupational therapy, podiatry and osteopathy. They are delivered mainly in the private sector. Some government funding of private allied health services is provided through insurance schemes and the private health insurance rebate. The Australian Government makes some allied health services available under Medicare to patients with particular needs – for example, people with chronic conditions and complex care needs. The Australian Government also funds the Workforce Incentive Program – Practice Steam, which supports general practices, Aboriginal Medical Services and Aboriginal Community Controlled Health Services with the cost of engaging eligible allied health professionals. Data on the number of Medicare rebated allied health services used per person and the availability of public allied health professionals by region is presented in tables 10A.10 and 10A.22, respectively.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003eCommunity health services\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eCommunity health services generally comprise multidisciplinary teams of health and allied health professionals who provide targeted health promotion, prevention and management services. Their aim is to protect the health and wellbeing of local populations, particularly people who have or are at risk of the poorest health and/or have the greatest economic and social needs, while taking pressure off the acute care health system. Governments (including local governments) provide community health services directly or indirectly by funding local health services and community organisations. There is no national strategy for community health services and service provision varies across jurisdictions.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eState and territory governments are responsible for most community health services. The Australian Government is primarily responsible for serving Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander communities, with state and territory governments also providing some funding.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003eMaternal and child health services\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eMaternal and child health services are funded by state and territory governments. They provide services including: parenting support (including antenatal and postnatal programs); early childhood nursing programs; disease prevention programs (including childhood immunisations); and early intervention and treatment programs related to child health and development. Some jurisdictions also provide specialist programs through child health services, including hearing screening programs, and mothers and babies residential programs.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003eAlcohol and other drug treatment\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eAlcohol and other drug treatment activities range from a brief intervention to long-term residential treatment. Types of treatment include withdrawal management, pharmacological treatment, counselling and rehabilitation. Selected data on these services is available in table 10A.13.\u003c/p\u003e"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"43:T5d4,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"\u003cp\u003eIn 2023-24, of the $51.5 billion government recurrent expenditure on primary and community health services, almost three-quarters was funded by the Australian Government and one-quarter by state, territory and local governments (table 10A.1). This included:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"e4e8aaf33e9c3007d98d9dfc8cf8f9fc9\"\u003e$12.8 billion for community health services (16.5% by the Australian Government and 83.5% by state, territory and local governments)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"e2273a1673752ec9936ed8960ee12c88f\"\u003e$2.5 billion for dental services (57.9% by the Australian Government and 42.1% by state, territory and local governments) (table 10A.6).\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhere more recent data is available, for 2024-25, Australian Government expenditure was:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"e7a457e253cd4a608cf00d57ef63e7a60\"\u003e$13.4 billion on general practice (table 10A.2)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"ec222400ca778382b17376c11a2de764f\"\u003e$19.5 billion through the PBS and RPBS on Section 85 prescription medicines filled at pharmacies (table 10A.3). A further 6.9 million services were subsidised under the RPBS (tables 10A.11–12).\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNationally in 2023-24 there were:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"e32917b97cf4e6c4f563a54ffdebb4801\"\u003e1,304 alcohol and other drug treatment agencies (31.2% identified as government providers) with a reported 240,958 closed treatment episodes (26.8% identified as government provided) (table 10A.13).\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli data-list-item-id=\"ebf32071aa5f2949a1768dffa0da40751\"\u003e213 Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander primary health services which provided 3.8 million episodes of healthcare (table 10A.14). For data that is not considered directly comparable, text includes relevant caveats and supporting commentary. \u003ca href=\"/ongoing/report-on-government-services/approach/performance-measurement\"\u003eSection 1\u003c/a\u003e discusses data comparability and completeness from a report-wide perspective. In addition to the contextual information for this service area (refer to Context tab), the report's statistical context (\u003ca href=\"/ongoing/report-on-government-services/approach/statistical-context\"\u003esection 2\u003c/a\u003e) contains data that may assist in interpreting the performance indicators presented in this section.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eImprovements to performance reporting for primary and community health services are ongoing and include identifying data sources to fill gaps in reporting for performance indicators and measures, and improving the comparability and completeness of data.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eOutputs\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOutputs are the services delivered (while outcomes are the impact of these services on the status of an individual or group) (refer to section 1). Medical practitioners who were on the vocational register on 16 June 2021 maintain their access to general practice items in the Medicare Benefits Schedule.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003cth scope=\"row\" id=\"table29468r16c1\"\u003eGeneral practitioner full time equivalent (GP FTE)\u003c/th\u003e\u003ctd headers=\"table29468r16c1 table29468r1c2\"\u003eGP FTE is a workforce specific method to estimate the workload of GPs. The method calculates a GP’s workload based on Medicare services claimed as well as patient and doctor factors that affect the duration of a consultation. One GP FTE represents a 40 hour week per week for 46 weeks of the year. For each Medicare provider, the measure attributes an estimate of the amount of time they have spent on their claims compared to what would be worked by a full-time GP, including billable time, non-billable time, and non-clinical time.\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003cth scope=\"row\" id=\"table29468r17c1\"\u003eGeneral practitioner (GP) Headcount\u003c/th\u003e\u003ctd headers=\"table29468r17c1 table29468r1c2\"\u003e\u003cp\u003eGP Headcount is a workforce specific method of headcount for GPs working in Australia (number of GPs).
